P_{j+1}

Figure 8.
Presentation by extension

A first presentation for
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})

We proceed to calculate presentations of the mixed twist groups
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}},{\leftmoon_{j}})
.
We follow the notations of
Figure

3
and (
2.5
).
Set

\tau_{-s}=\sigma_{1}^{\zeta_{p}\cdots\zeta_{s}},\quad 1\leq s\leq p

to replace
\tau_{2g+b-1+s}
for
1\leq s\leq p
.
Then the relative position of these
\tau_{r}
’s is shown in the top picture of
Figure

9
.
We recall another finite presentation in
[
QZ2
]
(with slight modification).

Theorem 2.16
.

[
QZ2
, Thm. 4.1]

Suppose that either
w\geq 5
, or
w=4
and
\operatorname{rk}\leq 2
.
The braid twist group
\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
has the following finite presentation.

•

Generators:
\sigma_{i}
and
\tau_{r}
for
1\leq i\leq w-1,-p\leq r(\neq 0)\leq 2g+b-1
.

•

Relations: the standard ones

(2.23)

\begin{cases}\operatorname{Co}(\sigma_{i},\sigma_{j})&\text{if $|i-j|>1$}\\
\operatorname{Br}(\sigma_{i},\sigma_{j})&\text{if $|i-j|=1$}\\
\operatorname{Co}(\tau_{r},\sigma_{i})&\text{if $i>2$}\\
\operatorname{Br}(\tau_{r},x)&\forall r\\
\operatorname{Br}(\tau_{r},y)&\forall r\\
\end{cases}

together with two extra ones

(2.24)

\begin{cases}\operatorname{Co}({\tau_{r}}^{y},{\tau_{s}}^{x})&\text{if $r<s<0$ or $s<0<r$,}\\
\operatorname{Co}({\tau_{r}}^{y},{\tau_{s}}^{x})&\text{if $0<s<r$ and $s\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{+}_{\leq g}$,}\\
\operatorname{Co}({\tau_{r}}^{\underline{y}},{\tau_{s}}^{x})&\text{if $0<s<r$ and $s+1\in 2\mathbb{Z}^{+}_{\leq g}$,}\end{cases}

for (cf.
[
QZ2
, Fig. 11]

x:=\tau_{-1}{}^{\underline{\sigma_{2}}}=\sigma_{2}^{\tau_{-1}},\quad y:=\sigma_{3}^{\underline{\sigma_{2}}}=\sigma_{2}^{\sigma_{3}}

and any
-p\leq s,r<2g+b
with
sr\neq 0
.

Denote by
\Sigma_{j}
the surface obtained form
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
by forgetting about the punctures

\{P_{s}\mid 1\leq s\leq j\}
.
Note that
\Sigma_{0}=\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
and
\Sigma_{p}\cong\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{0}
(as decorated surfaces).
The braid twist group
\operatorname{BT}(\Sigma_{j})
is a subgroup of
\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
with generators

(2.25)

\displaystyle G_{j}=\{\sigma_{i},\tau_{r}\mid 1\leq i\leq w-1,\ -p+j\leq r(\neq 0)\leq 2g+b-1\}

and same relations involving these generators.
Denote the set of relations by
\Lambda_{j}
.

The relative positions of the generators in
G_{0}
are shown in the top picture of
Figure

9

and there are four kinds of them:

•

\tau_{s}
for
{1\leq s\leq 2g}
correspond to arcs going around the genus.

•

\tau_{s}
for
{2g+1\leq s\leq 2g+b-1}
correspond to arcs going around the boundary components.

•

\tau_{-s}
for
1\leq s\leq p
correspond to arcs going around punctures.

•

\sigma_{i}
for
{1\leq i\leq w-1}
correspond to the ones connecting other decorations.

Figure 9.
Moving the generators

Our strategy is to moving the arc
\tau_{-s}
, for
1\leq s\leq p
, so that their endpoints are separated
the upper part of the top picture of
Figure

9
as shown in the bottom picture there.
The method of moving is by conjugation.
For instance, we begin with moving
\tau_{-1}
to
a_{1}
,
by conjugating
\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{w-1}
first, then by
\tau_{-2}
and
then by
\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{w-2}
.
Explicitly, we have

(2.26)

\displaystyle a_{1}=\tau_{-1}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\underline{\left(\sigma_{2}\cdots\sigma_{w-1}\right)\cdot\tau_{-2}\left(\sigma_{2}\cdots\sigma_{w-2}\right)}}

The change of the relations are easy to write down accordingly as
at each step we only conjugate by one of the generators.
Now we apply
Lemma

2.15
,
setting
(x,\eta,a,\zeta)=(\sigma_{w-2}.\sigma_{w-1},a_{1},\zeta_{1})
,
to get a presentation of the mixed twist group

\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}},{\leftmoon_{1}})=\langle\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}},{\leftmoon_{0}}),\zeta_{1}\rangle=\langle\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}),\zeta_{1}\rangle

from
Theorem

2.16
,
with the set of generators
(G_{0}\smallsetminus\{\tau_{-1}\})\cup\{\zeta_{1}\}=G_{1}\cup\{\zeta_{1}\}
.
Directly calculation shows that the relations are precisely
\Lambda_{1}
together with

\operatorname{Br}^{4}(\zeta_{1},\sigma_{w-1})\quad\text{and}\quad\operatorname{Co}(\zeta_{1},\alpha),\alpha\in G_{1}\smallsetminus\sigma_{w-1}.

Now we can proceed to move next
\tau_{-s}
and then include another
\zeta_{s}
in
\operatorname{LA}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
to
get a presentation inductively for

(2.27)

\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}},{\leftmoon_{j}})=\langle\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}},{\leftmoon_{j-1}}),\zeta_{j}\rangle.

Note that in order to apply
Lemma

2.15
properly, we need to move
\sigma_{w-s}
a bit,
e.g. by conjugating
\underline{\sigma_{w-s-1}\sigma_{w-s-2}}
(and move back afterward).
Similarly as above, the set of generators is
\{\zeta_{s}\mid 1\leq s\leq j\}\cup G_{j}
,
for the L-twist along some L-arcs
\zeta_{s}
, and the relations are precisely

\Lambda_{j}
together with

\operatorname{Br}^{4}(\zeta_{s},\beta_{s})\quad\text{and}\quad\operatorname{Co}(\zeta_{s},\alpha),\alpha\in G_{j}\smallsetminus\beta_{s};\quad 1\leq s\leq j,

where
\beta_{s}
is the only arc in
G_{j}
that intersect with
\zeta_{s}
(and with
\operatorname{Int}=1
).

Equivalence between presentations

For
s=p
, we obtain a presentation for
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
,
which compares with the presentation of
\operatorname{BT}(\Sigma_{p})=\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
,
the set of generators has extra
p
L-twists
\{\zeta_{s}\mid 1\leq s\leq p\}
,
the set of relations has extra relations:

\operatorname{Br}^{4}(\zeta_{s},\beta_{s})\quad\text{and}\quad\operatorname{Co}(\zeta_{s},\alpha),\alpha\in G_{j}\smallsetminus\beta_{s};\quad 1\leq s\leq p.

Since
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}\cong\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{0}
as decorated surfaces,
we have
\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta})\cong\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{0})=\operatorname{BT}(\mathrm{T}_{0}^{*})=\operatorname{BT}(\mathbb{T}^{*}_{\operatorname{CA}})
.
As shown in
[
QZ2
]
, the presentation of
\operatorname{BT}(\mathrm{T}_{0}^{*})
is in fact derived from
\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta})
.
Thus, the above presentation is equivalent to the one in
Theorem

2.12
.
∎

3.
Flip graphs and categorification

3.1.
Flip (exchange) graphs

Let
\mathrm{T}
be a triangulation of
\mathbf{S}
.
For any
\gamma
in
\mathrm{T}
,
the neighbourhood
N_{\mathrm{T}}(\gamma)
of
\gamma
are the union of all
\mathrm{T}
-triangles containing
\gamma
.
If
\gamma
is not a self-folded edge, there are exactly two
\mathrm{T}
-triangles containing
\gamma

and
N_{\mathrm{T}}(\gamma)
is a quadrilateral.
If
\gamma
is a self-folded edge in
\mathrm{T}
,
then
N_{\mathrm{T}}(\gamma)
is a self-folded triangle but still can be treated as quadrilateral
with two edges gluing together.

At each non self-folded edge
\gamma
of
\mathrm{T}
,
there is an operation
\mu_{\gamma}\colon\mathrm{T}-\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
,
known as the
usual (unoriented) flip
with respect to
\gamma
,
which produces another triangulation
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
from
\mathrm{T}
by replacing the diagonal
\gamma
of
N_{\mathrm{T}}(\gamma)

by the unique other diagonal
\gamma^{\prime}
of
N_{\mathrm{T}}(\gamma)
.

Denote by
\underline{\operatorname{EG}}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
the unoriented exchange graph of triangulations of
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}
,
whose vertices are triangulations and whose edges are usual flips.
The following is a well-known fact, cf.
[
Har
]
or
[
FST
, Thm. 3.10]
.

Theorem 3.1
.

\underline{\operatorname{EG}}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
is connected and its fundamental group is generated
by (unoriented) squares and pentagons, locally looks like the left and middle pictures of
Figure

12

(forgetting about the orientations of edges and the decorations there).

There is an oriented version of
\underline{\operatorname{EG}}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
.

Definition 3.2
.

Let
\mathrm{T}
be a triangulation of
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}
and
\gamma
be an open arc in
\mathrm{T}
.
There is an operation
\mu^{\sharp}_{\gamma}\colon\mathrm{T}\to\mathrm{T}^{\sharp}_{\gamma}
,
known as the
forward flip
with respect to
\gamma
,
which produces another triangulation
\mathrm{T}^{\sharp}_{\gamma}
from
\mathrm{T}

by replacing the arc
\gamma
with
\gamma^{\sharp}
.
Here,
\gamma^{\sharp}
is the open arc obtained from
\gamma

by moving each of its endpoint,
anticlockwise along boundaries of
N_{\mathrm{T}}(\gamma)
to the next marked point or puncture.

The forward flip operation also applies to decorated triangulations of
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
.
The inverse operation of which is the backward flip, denoted by
\mu^{\flat}_{\gamma}=(\mu^{\sharp}_{\gamma})^{-1}
.
We will use the notation
\pm\sharp=\mp\flat
from now on.

The three possible cases of forward flips are shown in
Figure

10
, for both
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
and
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}

with vertical correspondence by forgetful map.
The first two are
usual forward flips

and the last one (on the right) is the
self-folded forward flip
or
forward L-flip
for short. These oriented flips have been considered by many people before,
e.g.
[
GMN
]
and
[
IN
, Fig. 33/34]
.

Figure 10.
The forward flips with forgetful map

Denote by
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
and
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
the
flip (exchange) graph
of (decorated) triangulations of
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}
and
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
, respectively, where oriented edges are forward flips.
We use
\operatorname{FG}
instead of
\operatorname{EG}
to distinguish with the various other exchange graphs.

Figure 11.
Cluster exchange graph V.S. flip graph for once-punctured triangle

Example 3.3
.

If
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}
is a once punctured triangle,
then
\underline{\operatorname{EG}}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
and
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
are shown in the right picture of
Figure

11
,
where the unoriented/oriented version is in gray/purple, respectively.

Remark 3.4
.

When there is no punctures,
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
can be obtained from
\underline{\operatorname{EG}}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
by replacing each unoriented edge with a 2-cycle, as we did in
[
KQ2
]
.
However, with punctures,
\underline{\operatorname{EG}}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
is not
n
-regular (i.e. there are
n
edges at each vertex).
We fix this by including forward L-flip, which is a loop in
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
,
so that
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
is still
(n,n)
-regular
(i.e. there are
n
edges going out and in, respectively, at each vertex).
Moreover, one can obtained
\underline{\operatorname{EG}}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
from
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
by deleting loop edges and replacing every 2-cycle by an unoriented edge.

In
Appendix

B
, we will study the flip exchange graph with cluster style tagging at some of the punctures.
For instance, the left picture of
Figure

11
is the cluster exchange graph associated to
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}

(the famous associahedron).

As in the undecorated case, there a natural covering.

Lemma 3.5
.

The forgetful map induces a Galois covering
F^{\Delta}_{*}:\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\to\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
with covering group
\operatorname{SBr}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.

Proof.

Notice that although some of the edges are loops, the two graphs are both
(n,n)
-regular.
The rest follows the same as
[
KQ2
, Lem. 3.8]
.
∎

Usually,
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
is not connected.
The more tricky business is to determine the covering group when restricted to a connected component.
In the unpunctured case, this is stated properly as
[
BMQS
, Thm. 4.8]
, cf
[
Qy2
, Rem. 3.10]
.
In the punctured case, it is even harder (as the cluster theory is not applicable).

Fix any
\mathbb{T}\in\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.
Denote by
\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
the connected component of
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
containing
\mathbb{T}
.
So there is a covering

(3.1)

\displaystyle F^{\Delta}_{*}\mid_{\mathbb{T}}\colon\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\to\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}),

whose covering group is denoted by
\operatorname{MT}^{\text{\Sun}}\leq\operatorname{SBr}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.
We will show that
\operatorname{MT}^{\text{\Sun}}
is in fact
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
in
Corollary

5.7
,
which implies the following (using
Theorem

2.6
).

Corollary 3.6
.

The connected components of
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
is parameterized by

\operatorname{SBr}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})/\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\cong\operatorname{\bf H}_{1}(\mathbf{S}).

3.2.
The 3-Calabi-Yau category
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})

Recall that we assume that (
2.18
) which ensures that
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
admits decorated triangulations.
We will fix a triangulation
\mathbb{T}
,
which induces a natural grading on
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
induced by
\mathbb{T}
, cf.
[
CHQ
, § 2]
for more details.
Note that any triangulation and the dual graph of which carries natural gradings.
Denote by
\widetilde{\operatorname{CA}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
the set of graded arcs whose underlying arcs are in
\operatorname{CA}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
. Similarly for
\widetilde{\operatorname{LA}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
or a graded arc
\widetilde{\alpha}
.

For the preliminaries on simple tilting theory, see
Section

A.1
.

Let
\Gamma_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}}=\Gamma(Q_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}},W_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}})
be the
Ginzburg algebra
associated to the quiver with potential
(Q_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}},W_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}})

and
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
the
perfectly valued (or finite dimensional) derived category
of
\Gamma_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}}
.

The following is well-known (cf.
[
Ke1
]
):

\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
is 3-Calabi-Yau with a canonical heart
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}}
being (dg) module category of
\Gamma_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}}
.
Denote by
\operatorname{Sph}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
the set of
(reachable) spherical objects
in
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})

and
\operatorname{EG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}))
the principal component of exchange graph of hearts of
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.
Here is a summery of the results in
[
Chr
,
CHQ
]
, specified to our category.

Theorem 3.7
.

[
CHQ
]

There is an injective map:

(3.2)

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}\colon\widetilde{\operatorname{CA}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\bigsqcup\widetilde{\operatorname{LA}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})&\to&\operatorname{Obj}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}),\\
\widetilde{\alpha}&\mapsto&\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{\widetilde{\alpha}},\end{array}

satisfying

(3.3)

\operatorname{Int}^{d}(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{\beta})=\dim\operatorname{Hom}^{d}(\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{\widetilde{\alpha}},\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{\widetilde{\beta}}).

Moreover,
\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}
induces an isomorphism of oriented graphs:

(3.4)

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{*}\colon\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})&\xrightarrow{\cong}&\operatorname{EG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})),\\
\mathbb{T}&\mapsto&\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}},\\
\end{array}

such that
\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}}=\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}(\widetilde{\mathbb{T}^{*}})
.

In particular, for
\widetilde{\zeta}\in\widetilde{\operatorname{LA}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
,

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{\widetilde{\zeta}},\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{\widetilde{\zeta}})=\mathbf{k}
and when restricted to
\widetilde{\operatorname{CA}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
,
one has an injection

(3.5)

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}\colon\widetilde{\operatorname{CA}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})&\xrightarrow{1-1}&\operatorname{Sph}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})),\\
\widetilde{\eta}&\mapsto&\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{\widetilde{\eta}},\end{array}

Thus,
\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{\widetilde{\alpha}}
is rigid if and only if
\alpha
is in
\operatorname{CA}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.

Denote by
\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
the subgraph of
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
by deleting all L-flip edges.
Equivalently, it consists of flips
\mu^{\sharp}_{\gamma}:\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{T}^{\sharp}

such that the dual closed arc of the flipped open arc
\gamma
is in
\operatorname{CA}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
(not in
\operatorname{LA}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
).
Similarly for other variation of
\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}
.
We get the following corollary.

Corollary 3.8
.

The isomorphism
\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{*}
in (
3.4
) restricts to an isomorphism

(3.6)

\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{*}\colon\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{EG}_{\circledR}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})).

Note that for any
\mathbb{T}\in\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
, there are exactly
\#\mathbb{T}^{*}_{\operatorname{CA}}
edges coming in and going out.

Two of the key features of the categorical side are

•

the symmetry triangulated shift
[1]
and

•

the natural partial order on hearts.

Under the correspondences in
Theorem

3.7
,
we can also use
[1]
and
\leq
on the topological side for decorated triangulations of
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
.

Remark 3.9
.

For closed arcs, the shift correspond to grading shift.
The map
X
also extends to open arc and the alternative version of (
3.4
) is that

X(\mathbb{T})
is in fact a silting set in the perfect derived category
\operatorname{per}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
,
which is the Koszul dual of
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}}
in
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.
See
[
Qy3
,
Chr
,
CHQ
]
for more discussion.

On open arcs, the shift corresponds to the
universal rotation

\widetilde{\rho}\in\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.
More precisely, for any boundary component
\partial\in\partial\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
,
there is a rotation/operation
\rho_{\partial}
that moves each marked point to the next consecutive one:

\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\rho_{1}
\rho_{2}

These rotations are roots of the Dehn twists, i.e.
\rho_{\partial}^{m_{\partial}}=\operatorname{D}_{\partial}
.
And the universal rotation is the product of all:

(3.7)

\widetilde{\rho}\colon=\prod_{\partial\subset\partial\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}}\rho_{\partial}.

In particular, we have

\widetilde{\rho}\big(X(\mathbb{T})\big)=X(\mathbb{T})[1].

Notice that in each long dumbbell/symmetric hexagon in (
15
),
the source triangulated digon is the universal rotation of the sink triangulated digon.

3.3.
The fundamental domain

Denote by
\operatorname{EG}_{\circledR}^{\circ}[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}[1]]
the connected component in
\operatorname{EG}_{\circledR}[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}[1]]
containing
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}
.
By the isomorphism (
3.4
), we have

\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]=(\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{*})^{-1}(\operatorname{EG}_{\circledR}^{\circ}[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}[1]])

for
\mathbb{T}_{0}=(\operatorname{X}^{\text{\Sun}}_{*})^{-1}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0})
.

Denote by
\overline{\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]}^{\circlearrowleft}
the graph obtained from
\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]
by adding a reverse edge for each of its edges and adding
\#(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})^{*}_{\operatorname{LA}}=\#\leftmoon(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
loops at each vertex
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
.

Theorem 3.10
.

Suppose that
\mathbb{T}_{0}
is admissible.
Then there is a natural isomorphism

(3.8)

\displaystyle\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})/\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\cong\overline{\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]}^{\circlearrowleft}.

Proof.

Firstly, we claim that
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\cdot\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]\subset\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.
This is because the braid twist/L-twist
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
can be realized as either a composition of flips or a flip on its own.
More precisely, for any
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
, one has (cf.
u^{2}
and
s
in
Figure

15
, respectively)

(3.9)

\displaystyle(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})_{\gamma}^{\pm 2\sharp}=B_{\eta}^{\mp}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime}),

(3.10)

\displaystyle(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})_{\gamma}^{\pm\sharp}=L_{\zeta}^{\mp}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime}).

Here,
\eta
or
\zeta
in
(\mathbb{T}^{\prime})^{*}
is the dual of the arc
\gamma\in\mathbb{T}^{\prime}

and
(-)^{\pm 2\sharp}
means the iterated flips.
Then one can write down explicitly that the twist
\Psi(T_{0})
can be expressed as a sequence of flips staring from
T_{0}
in the same way as the formula
[
KQ1
, (8.4)]
, for any
\Psi\in\operatorname{MT}(\mathbb{T}_{0})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.

Secondly, we claim that
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\cdot\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]\supset\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.
We only need to how that if
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}=\Xi(\mathbb{T}_{1})
is in
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\cdot\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]
for
\Xi\in\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
and
\mathbb{T}_{1}\in\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]
,
then so are the any flips of
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
.

Equivalently, we only need to show that any flips of
\mathbb{T}_{1}
are still in
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\cdot\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]
if
\mathbb{T}_{1}
is.
This follows as below:

•

For any
\eta\in(\mathbb{T}_{1})^{*}_{\operatorname{CA}}
with
\gamma=\eta^{*}\in\mathbb{T}_{1}
,
exactly one of the flips
(\mathbb{T}_{1})^{\pm\sharp}_{\gamma}
is in
\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]
by
Lemma

A.2
(via the identification (
3.6
)).
The other flip will be one of the braid twist
B_{\eta}^{\pm}
of which by formula (
3.9
).

•

For any
\zeta\in\mathbb{T}^{*}_{\operatorname{LA}}
with
\gamma=\eta^{*}\in\mathbb{T}_{1}
,
the flips
(\mathbb{T}_{1})^{\pm\sharp}_{\gamma}
are precisely the L-twists by (
3.10
).

Hence
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\cdot\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]=\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
as sets.

Finally, the formulae (
3.9
) implies that each edge in
\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
needs to be doubled and (
3.10
) implies that there are certain loops need to be added, so that
one matches
\overline{\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]}^{\circlearrowleft}
with
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
precisely as graphs.
∎

Recall that there is a covering (
3.1
) so that

(3.11)

\displaystyle\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})/\operatorname{MT}^{\text{\Sun}}=\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}).

More precisely,
\operatorname{MT}^{\text{\Sun}}
is the subgroup of
\operatorname{SBr}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
consisting of elements
f\in\operatorname{SBr}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
satisfying
f(\mathbb{T})\in\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.
Thus,
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\leq\operatorname{MT}^{\text{\Sun}}
and, by the conjugation formula (
2.3
),
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\lhd\operatorname{MT}^{\text{\Sun}}
.
But at the moment, we only have the following commutative diagram,

(3.12)

\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
\overline{\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]}^{\circlearrowleft}
\big/\operatorname{MT}^{\text{\Sun}}
\big/\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
\operatorname{Ni}

for any admissible
\mathbb{T}_{0}
, where
\operatorname{Ni}=\operatorname{MT}^{\text{\Sun}}/\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.

In
Section

5.2
, we will show that
\operatorname{MT}^{\text{\Sun}}=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
and
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})=\overline{\operatorname{FG}_{\text{\tiny{$\copyright$}}}^{\circ}[\mathbb{T}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0}[1]]}^{\circlearrowleft}
.

4.
Flip groupoids and flip twist groups

In
[
KQ2
]
, we introduce the cluster exchange groupoids as the enhancement of cluster exchange graphs,
using the cluster combinatorics to produce generalization of braid groups.
Naturally, in the unpunctured surface case, as well as Dynkin case, we identified
cluster braid groups with braid twist groups and spherical twist groups.

In this section, we introduce the flip groupoids as refinements of Cayley graphs of mixed twist groups,
with the same nature above.
However, the square/pentagon/dumbbell relations there are not enough here.
New phenomenon appears due to the loop edges in
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
.

4.1.
Relations in cluster exchange graphs

Classical square and pentagon (relations)

Given a triangulation
\mathrm{T}
in
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
with any two vertices
i,j\in Q_{\mathrm{T}}
,
there is the following local full subgraph in
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
:

(4.1)

Suppose that there is no arrow from
i
to
j
.
If there is at most one arrow from
j
to
i
in
Q_{\mathrm{T}}
,
then such a subgraph completes into a double square or a double pentagon,
depending on if the numbers of arrow is zero or one, as shown below.

(4.2)

\bullet
x
y
\bullet
x
y
\bullet
x
y
\bullet
x
y
\otimes
\mathrm{T}=\;
\odot
\Big/_{x^{2}=y^{2}\;,}
\bullet
x
y
\bullet
x
y
\bullet
x
y
\bullet
x
y
\bullet
x
y
\Big/_{x^{2}=y^{3}\;.}
\otimes
\mathrm{T}=\;
\odot

The
square/pentgon relation
(at
\mathrm{T}
with respect to
i,j
)
is the relations
x^{2}=y^{h}
staring at
\mathrm{T}
(where
h=2
for square and
h=3
for pentagon).
In fact, by the symmetry, all
x^{2}=y^{h}
are this type of relations.

Note that these relations are well-known, cf.
[
Kra
,
Qy1
]
.
They are not exactly the ones in
Theorem

3.1
, but the oriented version.

Figure 12.
Square, pentagon and S-DB in
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})

Original short dumbbell (S-DB) relations

[
KQ2
, § 2]

Given a triangulation
\mathrm{T}
in
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
with two vertices
i,j
in
Q_{\mathrm{T}}

such that the corresponding open arcs in
\mathrm{T}
are not self-folded edges.
Suppose that there is no arrow from
i
to
j
as above and we still have (
4.1
).
We name the edges/flips
x
and
y
(omitting the subscripts for simplicity).
When there are arrows in one direction (between
i
and
j
),
then
x
and
y
can be distinguished as being forward mutation at either the tail or head of arrows.

(4.3)
