Remark 7.8

(Change of signs by L-flips and L-twists)
.

On the one hand, an L-flip naturally acts on the set of signs
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}

by changing the sign of the isolated puncture of the L-flip.
Thus, any L-flip in
\mu({\rightmoon})
changes precisely one sign.
On the other hand,
the quotient map
\pi_{\varepsilon}\colon\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\to\operatorname{\bf H}_{1}(\text{\YinYang})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}
provides a sign for each element in
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
Thus, any elements
L\in\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
also maps to a sign
\pi_{\varepsilon}[L]
, where
[L]
is the image of
L
in
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
In particular, an L-twist
L_{\zeta}
corresponding a sign in
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}
with precisely one minus at the puncture enclosed by
\zeta
, for any
\zeta\in\operatorname{LA}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}},{\rightmoon})
.
We can also view
L\in\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
acts on
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}
by
L(\epsilon)=\pi_{\varepsilon}[L]\cdot\epsilon
.
Note that we use
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=(\{\pm 1\},\cdot)
.

Construction 7.9
.

The quotient graph
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}
-covering of
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
.
Thus, as a set,
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
can be identified with
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}
.
In other word, each vertex in
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
can be represented by a signed triangulation
\mathrm{T}^{\times}=(\mathrm{T},\epsilon)
.

Recall that
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})
is obtained from
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
by killing all L-flips in
\mu({\rightmoon})
.
Then
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}
is a subgraph of
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
and the missing edges
are precisely of the form
(\mathrm{T},\epsilon)\xrightarrow{\mu}(\mathrm{T},\mu(\epsilon))
,
where
\mathrm{T}\xrightarrow{\mu}\mathrm{T}
is an L-flip in
\mu({\rightmoon})
with isolated puncture
P\in{\rightmoon}
,
and the sign
\mu(\epsilon)
only differ with
\epsilon
at
P
.

Compare to the construction of
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
, we can obtain it from
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
by

Operation-
{\rightmoon}
:

squeezing all squares along parallel edges that are in
\mu({\rightmoon})
.

Equivalently, Operation-
{\rightmoon}
identifies triangulations differ by an L-flip in
\mu({\rightmoon})

and identifies edges if both their endpoints are identified.

More precisely,
consider a square in
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
with
L-flips
\mathrm{T}_{i}\xrightarrow{\mu_{0}}\mathrm{T}_{i}
in
\mu({\rightmoon})
and a flip
\mathrm{T}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mu_{\gamma}}\mathrm{T}_{2}
.
Then there is the following full subgraph

(7.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}_{1},\epsilon_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}_{2},\epsilon_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}_{1},\epsilon_{1}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{\prime})}

in
\operatorname{EG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
. Note that
\epsilon_{i}^{\prime}=\mu_{0}(\epsilon_{i})
.

a).

If
\mathrm{T}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mu_{\gamma}}\mathrm{T}_{2}
is not in
\mu({\rightmoon})
,
(
7.10
) is squeezes into a 2-cycle

(7.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}_{1},\epsilon_{1}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T}_{2},\epsilon_{2}^{\prime}).}

b).

If the flip
\mathrm{T}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mu_{\gamma}}\mathrm{T}_{2}
is also an L-flip in
\mu({\rightmoon})
,
(
7.10
) is squeezed into a vertex.

The advantage of introducing such an operation is that it also applies to
\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
,
which resulting a graph, denoted by
\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
The difference is that in Case a), the full subgraph (
7.10
) changes to

(7.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{T}_{1}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{T}_{2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{T}_{1}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{T}_{2}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\gamma}}

in
\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
, where
[\mathbb{T}_{i}]
is a triangulation in
\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
,
i.e. an equivalent class of triangulation up to
\operatorname{L}^{2}({\rightmoon})
, which carries a sign naturally by
Remark

7.8
.
It squeezes into just an edge

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{T}_{1}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{T}_{2}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\gamma}}
.

As Operation-
{\rightmoon}
is compatible with the
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
-action on
\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
,
we obtain the lemma below.

Lemma 7.10
.

\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
is a
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
-covering of
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
, that fits into the left half of the diagram in
Figure

24

The following conjecture is one of the reason we construct
\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.

Conjecture 7.11
.

The graph isomorphism in
Proposition

B.13
upgrades to
\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\cong\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
.
Equivalently, the isomorphism in
Theorem

B.15
upgrades to the fact that

\operatorname{Stab}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
is the universal cover of
\operatorname{Quad}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.

7.5.
Flip groupoids for DMSx

The (quotient) flip graphs in
Figure

24
on the left hand side can be upgraded into flip groupoids naturally by quotienting the groupoid
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
(as mentioned above).
Moreover, the effect of Operation-
{\rightmoon}
on (generating) relations of flip groupoid in
Section

4.1
is as follows (we only list the ones that get changed):

•

A square can be either squeeze into a vertex or an edge.

•

A L-DB or a Sym-Hex in
Figure

14
can be squeeze into a square
uv=vu

if
s
and
r
contracts. See
Figure

15
, where
all four hexagons become squares, if the puncture is in
{\rightmoon}
.

Thus, we obtain various flip groupoids.

Definition 7.12
.

•

The groupoid
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
is obtained from
\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})

by composing inherited square/pentagon/S-DB/L-DB/Sym-Hex relations
from the quotient groupoid
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})=\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})/\operatorname{L}^{2}({\rightmoon})
.

•

The groupoid
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
is obtained from
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})

by composing inherited square/pentagon/S-DB/L-DB/Sym-Hex relations
from the quotient groupoid
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})=\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})/\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
.

Then
Lemma

7.10
upgrades to that
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
is a
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
-covering of
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
Next, we claim that
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
is simply connected.

Proposition 7.13
.

\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
is simply connected so that
\pi_{1}\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\cong\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
.

Proof.

A loop
p
in the quotient
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
lifts to a path

\widetilde{p}\colon\mathbb{T}_{1}\to\mathbb{T}_{2}
such that
[\mathbb{T}_{1}]=[\mathbb{T}_{2}]
up to
\operatorname{L}^{2}({\rightmoon})
.
Since
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
is simply connected,

p
is non-trivial if and only if
\mathbb{T}_{1}\neq\mathbb{T}_{2}
and the homotopy class of
p
only depends on the endpoints
\mathbb{T}_{i}
of
\widetilde{p}
.

We only need to show that any such a loop
p
get killed during Operation-
{\rightmoon}
.
By induction, we only need to consider the case when
\mathbb{T}_{2}=L_{\zeta}(\mathbb{T}_{1})
for some
\zeta\in\operatorname{LA}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}},{\rightmoon})
.
Let
P
be the puncture enclosed by
\zeta
.
Let
\mathbb{T}_{0}
be an admissible triangulation in
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})

and
\zeta_{0}
in
\mathbb{T}_{0}^{*}
is the L-arc in the dual graph enclosing
P
.
By
Lemma

2.14
, there exists
b\in\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})\lhd\operatorname{MT}(\mathbb{T}_{0})
such that
\zeta=b(\zeta_{0})
.
Choose a path
\widetilde{p_{0}}
in
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
connecting
T_{1}
and
b(\mathbb{T}_{0})=\mathbb{T}
.
So
\zeta
is in the dual graph of
\mathbb{T}
, which is dual to some arc
\gamma\in\mathbb{T}
.
Hence there are L-flips
\mathbb{T}\xrightarrow{\mu_{\zeta}}L_{\zeta}(\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\mu_{\zeta}}L^{2}_{\zeta}(\mathbb{T})
.
Then we obtain a loop in
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
:

(7.13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{2}=\zeta(\mathbb{T}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\zeta}(\mathbb{T})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{2}_{\zeta}(\mathbb{T}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{2}_{\zeta}(\widetilde{p_{0}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\zeta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\zeta}}

Thus
p
in
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})
equals the conjugate of
the 2-cycle
\mu_{\zeta}^{2}
by the image
p_{0}
of
\widetilde{p_{0}}
.
On the other hand, the 2-cycle
\mu_{\zeta}^{2}
is killed by Operation-
{\rightmoon}
, we see that

p
also becomes trivial in
\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
as required.
∎

Now we have a fancy version of
Theorem

7.7
, which is essentially equivalent by (
7.5
).
It is the
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}
-version of
Theorem

6.9
(together with (
6.21
) in
Corollary

6.11
).

Corollary 7.14
.

The fundamental group of
\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
is

(7.14)

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\cong\pi_{1}\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})=\ker\operatorname{AJ}^{\text{\YinYang}}

and hence
\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\ker\operatorname{AJ}
that fits into the octahedron (
7.15
).

(7.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{SBr}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\bf H}_{1}(\mathbf{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}\operatorname{Quad}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\bf H}_{1}(\mathbf{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{AJ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\Delta}_{*}}

Proof.

Firstly, similar to (
6.7
), any square, pentagon, S-DB, L-DB or Sym-Hex is contractible in

\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
(one can either use the same proof for
\operatorname{FQuad}
or passing to
\operatorname{Stab}
using
Theorem

B.15
).
Thus,
Lemma

B.14
upgrades to a surjective map

\wp_{*}\colon\pi_{1}\operatorname{\mathcal{FG}}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\to\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}).

Then combining
Proposition

7.13
and
Theorem

7.7
, (
7.14
) follows.
Similar to (
6.21
), we then obtain (
7.15
) using
Lemma

6.10
.
∎

Appendix A
Tilting and stability structures

A.1.
Simple tilting theory

We will first recall basic notions and notations about triangulated categories,
tilting theory and the 3-Calabi-Yau categories associated to a DMSp.
Then we review the categorification of flip graphs from
[
CHQ
]
, cf.
[
Qy2
,
Chr
]
.

We work over algebraically closed field
\mathbf{k}
and
\mathbf{k}
-linear additive categories.
A category usually means an abelian or triangulated category.
Most stuffs in this subsection are classical, we refer to
[
B1
,
KQ1
,
Qy1
]
for general references.

We write
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{T}\perp\mathcal{F}
for a torsion pair in a category
\mathcal{C}
.
An object
S
in
\mathcal{C}
is
rigid
if
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(S,S)=0
.

For an abelian category
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
, denote by
\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
the set of simples of
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
.
Such an
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
is called
finite
if
\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
is finite and generates
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}

(so that
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
is a length category).

Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}
be a triangulated category.
A
t-structure

\mathcal{P}
of
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}
is the torsion part of a torsion pair
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}=\mathcal{P}\perp\mathcal{P}^{\perp}

such that
\mathcal{P}[1]\subset\mathcal{P}
.
It is bounded if

\mathcal{D}=\displaystyle\bigcup_{i,j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}[i]\cap\mathcal{P}[j],

The
heart
of a bounded t-structure
\mathcal{P}
is
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{P}^{\perp}[1]\cap\mathcal{P}
, which
is abelian and determines
\mathcal{P}
uniquely.
For any object
E\in\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}
, there is a HN-filtration

(A.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0=E_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{l-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{l}=E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{l}}

with factors
F_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}[k_{i}]
for
k_{1}>k_{2}>\cdots>k_{l}
.

Given a torsion pair
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{T}\perp\mathcal{F}
in
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
,
there is a new heart
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\sharp}
, known as the
forward (HRS) tilt
of
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
with respect to the torsion pair
(cf.
[
HRS
]
), which admits a torsion pair
\mathcal{F}[1]\perp\mathcal{T}
(and hence determined by them).
The dual notion of forward tilting is backward tilting.
For
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{T}\perp\mathcal{F}
, one has
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\flat}=\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\sharp}[-1]=\mathcal{F}\perp\mathcal{T}[-1]
.

There is a natural partial order on the set of hearts, i.e.

(A.2)

\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{1}\leq\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{2}\;\Longleftrightarrow\;\mathcal{P}_{2}\subset\mathcal{P}_{1}\;\Longleftrightarrow\;\mathcal{P}^{\perp}_{1}\subset\mathcal{P}^{\perp}_{2}.

Another way to characterize forward tilts of a heart is that they are precisely all heart in the interval

[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}[1]]
, i.e. (cf.
[
KQ1
, § 3]
)

(A.3)

\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}=\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\sharp}\text{ w.r.t. $\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{T}\perp\mathcal{F}$ if and only if}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}\leq\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\leq\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}[1].

Indeed, in such a case, one has
\mathcal{T}=\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\cap\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
and
\mathcal{F}=\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}[-1]\cap\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
.

Definition A.1
.

[
KQ1
]

A forward tilting
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\sharp}
with respect to
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}=\mathcal{T}\perp\mathcal{F}
is
simple
if the corresponding torsion part
\mathcal{F}
is generated by a single simple
S\in\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
.
Denote by
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\sharp}_{S}
the simple forward tilt in this case and by

\mu_{S}^{\sharp}\colon S\to\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\sharp}_{S}
the simple forward tilting.

A backward tilting is simple if the corresponding torsion part is generated by a single simple
S

and the backward tilt will be denoted by
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\flat}_{S}
.
The backward tilting is the inverse operation of forward tilting and we have

\mu_{S[-1]}^{\sharp}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\flat}_{S}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
.

The
exchange graph (of hearts)

\operatorname{EG}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
of a triangulated category
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}
is the directed graph whose vertices are hearts and whose edges are simple forward tilting.
When
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}
admits a canonical heart
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
,
denote by
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
the principal component of
\operatorname{EG}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
, i.e. the connected component containing
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
.

We often interested in intervals of in
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
.
Denote by
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{1},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{2}]
the connected component of the full subgraph
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})\cap[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{1},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{2}]
containing
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
.

There are couple of simple tilting formulae,
e.g
[
KQ1
, Prop. 5.4]
for the rigid case and
[
CHQ
, § A.2]
for a slight generalization.

In many good scenarios,
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
will consists of finite hearts and the formula apply to any of its edges.
In that case,
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
is
(n,n)
-regular, e.g. when
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}=\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}^{b}(Q)
for an acyclic quiver
Q
or
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}=\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{N}(Q)
for the
N
-Calabi-Yau version of
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}^{b}(Q)
.

As rigid simple tilting is very nice and we introduce the rigid exchange graph

\operatorname{EG}_{\circledR}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
to be the subgraph of
\operatorname{EG}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
whose edge are simple tilting with respect to rigid simples.
Similar for other variation of
\operatorname{EG}
, e.g.
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}
,
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{1},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{2}]
, etc.
Note that the formula above only ensures the finiteness of the tilted heart, not the rigidity of the tilted simples.
Hence,
\operatorname{EG}_{\circledR}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
may not be equal
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
even if it is
(n,n)
-regular.

We recall another technical result from
[
KQ1
, Lem. 3.8]
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}\in\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}[1]]
. Then, for any rigid
S\in\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
,
it must be in one of
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}
or
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}[1]
and

•

{\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}}^{\sharp}_{S}\in\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}[1]]

if and only if
S\in\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}
if and only if
S\notin\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}[1]
.

•

{\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}}^{\flat}_{S}\in\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}[1]]

if and only if
S\notin\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}
if and only if
S\in\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}[1]
.

In other words, exactly one of
{\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}}^{\pm\sharp}_{S}
is still in
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{0}[1]]
.

A.2.
Stability structures on triangulated categories

Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}
be a triangulated category.
Recall the basic notion and result from
[
B1
]
.

Definition A.3
.

[
B1
]

A
stability condition

\sigma=(Z,\mathcal{P})
consists of a
central charge

Z\in\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{K}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}),\mathbb{C})
and a
\mathbb{R}
-family
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{R}}=\{\mathcal{P}(\phi)\mid\phi\in\mathbb{R}\}
of additive (in fact, abelian) subcategories (known as the
slicing
), satisfying (cf.
[
B1
]
for more details):

•

if
0\neq E\in\mathcal{P}(\phi)
,
then
Z(E)=m(E)\exp(\phi\pi\mathbf{i})
for some
m(E)\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
;

•

\mathcal{P}(\phi+1)=\mathcal{P}(\phi)[1]
, for all
\phi\in\mathbb{R}
;

•

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{P}(\phi_{1}),\mathcal{P}(\phi_{2}))=0
if
\mathcal{P}(\phi_{1})>\mathcal{P}(\phi_{2})
;

•

each nonzero object
E\in\mathcal{D}
admits a
Harder-Narashimhan
filtration (
A.1
)
with factors
F_{i}\in\mathcal{P}(\phi_{i})
for
\phi_{1}>\cdots>\phi_{l}
.
The upper and lower phases of
E
are
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{l}
respectively.

•

the so-called
support property
.

Objects in the slice
\mathcal{P}(\phi)
are called
semistable
objects with phase
\phi
;
simple objects in
\mathcal{P}(\phi)
is called
stable
objects with phase
\phi
.
For an interval
J
, denote by
\mathcal{P}(J)
the subcategory consisting of objects whose upper and lower phases
are in
J
.
The
heart

\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}
of a stability condition
\sigma=(Z,\mathcal{P})
is the abelian category
\mathcal{P}(0,1]
.

Theorem A.4
.

[
B1
, Thm. 1]

All stability conditions on a triangulated category
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}

form a complex manifold, denoted by
\operatorname{Stab}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
, with local coordinate given by the central charge.

There are two natural actions on
\operatorname{Stab}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
, the
\mathbb{C}
-action given by

s\cdot(Z,\mathcal{P})=(Z\cdot e^{-\mathbf{i}\pi s},\mathcal{P}_{\operatorname{Re}(s)}),

for
\mathcal{P}_{x}(\phi)=\mathcal{P}(\phi+x)
and the
\operatorname{Aut}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}
-action given by

\Phi(Z,\mathcal{P})=\big(Z\circ\Phi^{-1},\Phi(\mathcal{P})\big).

Generic-finite component

For a finite heart
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
,
the stability conditions supported in
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
(i.e. with
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}=\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
) is parameterized by
the central charges
Z(S)\in\mathbf{H}
for
S\in\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
.
Thus there is a cell/subspace
\operatorname{U}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})\cong\mathbf{H}^{\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}}
in
\operatorname{Stab}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
consisting of
those stability conditions supported on
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
.

For each tilting
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}={\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}}^{\sharp}_{S}
,
there is co-dimensional one wall where
Z(S)\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
,

\partial^{\sharp}_{S}\operatorname{U}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})=\partial^{\flat}_{S[1]}\operatorname{U}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime})=\overline{\operatorname{U}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})}\cap\overline{\operatorname{U}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime})}.

For a connected component
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}
in
\operatorname{EG}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
, define

B_{0}(\operatorname{EG}^{\circ})=\bigcup_{\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}\in\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}}\operatorname{U}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})

and

B_{2}(\operatorname{EG}^{\circ})=B_{0}(\Gamma)\cup\bigcup_{\begin{smallmatrix}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}\in\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}\\
S\in\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}\end{smallmatrix}}\partial^{\sharp}_{S}\operatorname{U}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}).

A connected component
\operatorname{Stab}^{\circ}
in
\operatorname{Stab}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})
is
generic-finite

if
\operatorname{Stab}^{\circ}=\mathbb{C}\cdot B_{2}(\operatorname{EG}^{\circ})
for some connected component
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}
of
\operatorname{EG}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}})

consisting of finite hearts.

Appendix B
Marked surfaces with mixed punctures & vortices

Recall that there is a partition
\text{\Sun}=\leftmoon\cup{\rightmoon}
.
We add extra
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetry to punctures in
{\rightmoon}
, call them
vortices

and use
☯
to denote the set of vortices.
Let
\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}
be the marked surface with mixed punctures & vortices (MSx).
More precisely, what this means is:

•

Categorically, we will add terms around each vortex in
☯
for quivers with potential associated to triangulations and deform
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
into
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.

•

Geometrically, we will allow collisions between simple zeros in
\Delta
and double poles in
☯
,
so that we partially compactify the moduli space
\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
into
\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
by adding strata with simple poles from collision (with orbifolding).

Then we show that the corresponding quotient space
\operatorname{Stab}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})/\operatorname{Aut}^{\circ}
is isomorphic to
the unframed version of
\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
When
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
is fully deformed, i.e. taking
\text{\YinYang}=\text{\Sun}
,
We will call the corresponding MSx by MSv (with notation
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}}
).
In such a case,
\operatorname{Quad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})
becomes the moduli space in
[
BS
]
.

Remark B.1
.

We assume the following when considering MSx:

•

\partial\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}\neq\varnothing
.

•

n\geq 2
, where
n
is defined in (
2.17
).

The main results are covered in
[
KQ2
]
when
\text{\Sun}=\varnothing

and hence we usually assume
\text{\Sun}\neq\varnothing
in the proofs.

B.1.
Cluster-ish combinatorics for MSx

Signed triangulations of MSx

A
signed triangulation

\mathrm{T}^{\times}
of a MSx
\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}
consists of
an (ideal) triangulation
\mathrm{T}
of MSp
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}
with a
sign

(B.1)

\epsilon\colon\text{\YinYang}\to\{\pm 1\}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

The set of signs can be identified with
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}
.
Two signed triangulations
\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{1}=(\mathrm{T}_{1},\epsilon_{1})
and
\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{2}=(\mathrm{T}_{2},\epsilon_{2})
are equivalent if

•

\mathrm{T}_{1}=\mathrm{T}_{2}
and

•

\epsilon_{1}(V)=\epsilon_{2}(V)
, for any vortex
V
that is not in a self-folded triangle.

Namely, we have the (local) equivalence shown in
Figure

25
.
In other words, the sign at a vortex which is in a self-folded triangulation can be chosen arbitrarily
without changing the equivalent class of the signed triangulation.

\bullet
\blacksquare
☯
+
\sim
\bullet
\blacksquare
☯
-

Figure 25.
Equivalent signed triangulations (local)

There is a forward flip between signed triangulations
\mu^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{1}\to\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{2}

if one can choose the (representatives of) signs
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{2}
,
such that there is a usual forward flip between the corresponding triangulations
\mu^{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{T}_{1}\to\mathrm{T}_{2}

or there is a forward L-flip between the corresponding triangulations, where the corresponding isolated puncture is not a vortex.
For more details, cf.
[
FST
]
.
Then we have the corresponding notation of (oriented) cluster-ish exchange graph
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
for MSx.

Alternatively/concretely,
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
can be constructed as follows.
Note that when we write
{\rightmoon}
instead of
☯
,
we mean there is no
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-symmetry (i.e. signs) attached.

•

Denote by
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})
the flip graph for
\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}}
, which is a subgraph of
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
,
by deleting L-flips where the corresponding enclosed puncture is in fact a vortex.

•

For each sign
\epsilon\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}
, take a copy
\operatorname{FG}^{\epsilon}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})=\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})\times\{\epsilon\}
.

•

We take the union of
\operatorname{FG}^{\epsilon}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})
, for all
\epsilon\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}
,
and identify vertices/edges by the equivalent relation
\sim
above.

So we have

(B.2)

\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}}\operatorname{FG}^{\epsilon}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})=\big(\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}\big)\big/\sim.

There is also an unoriented version of
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
,
which can be obtained from it by deleting all loop edges and replacing each 2-cycle with an unoriented edge.
Equivalently, one has

(B.3)

\underline{\operatorname{EG}}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\bigcup_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}}\underline{\operatorname{EG}}^{\epsilon}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})=\big(\underline{\operatorname{EG}}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}\big)\big/\sim.

For instance, the left picture of
Figure

11
shows the building process of
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})

for a once-puncture triangle with
\text{\YinYang}=\text{\Sun}
.
When
\text{\YinYang}=\text{\Sun}
, we have
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{CEG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
and
\underline{\operatorname{EG}}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})=\underline{\operatorname{CEG}}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
are the
oriented and unoriented cluster exchange graph associated to
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}
respectively.

Building on
Theorem

3.1
,
we have the following result as a variation of
Theorem

3.1
, using the same proof of
[
FST
, Thm.10.2]
.

Proposition B.2
.

\pi_{1}\underline{\operatorname{EG}}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
is generated by squares and pentagons,
which locally look like the (unoriented) squares/pentagons in
Figure

12
,
or the one in
Figure

26
.

Figure 26.
The square involving vortex

Mutation, reduction and right-equivalence

There are the notions of
right-equivalence
,
reduction
and
mutation
introduced by
[
DWZ
]
, cf.
[
KY
, § 2.1]
for more details.

•

Two quivers with potential are right-equivalent
if there is an algebra isomorphism between the corresponding completed path algebras
such that it is the identity when restricted to the vertices
and induces cyclically equivalence between the potentials.

•

The reduction is an operation on right-equivalent classes of quivers with potential.
It reduces a quiver with potential by killing some 2-cycles (and change the potential according).
A quiver with potential
(Q,W)
is called
reduced
if
\partial_{a}W
is contained in
\mathfrak{m}^{2}
for any
a\in Q_{1}
.
By
[
DWZ
, Thm. 4.6]
, the reduction is well-defined on the right-equivalence classes of quivers with potential.

•

The mutation
\mu_{i}
is an operation on a quiver with potential
(Q,W)
at a vertex
i
of
Q
;
it produces another quiver with potential.
By
[
DWZ
, Thm. 5.2 and Thm. 5.7]
, the mutation is also well-defined up to right-equivalence,
which is an involution.

Quivers with potential associated to triangulated MSx

Let
\mathrm{T}^{\times}=(\mathrm{T},\epsilon)
be a signed triangulation.
Define a quiver with potential
(\widehat{Q_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}},\widehat{W_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}})
as follows:

•

The vertices are open arcs in
\mathrm{T}
.

•

For each triangle
T
of
\mathrm{T}
, which is not a self-folded triangle enclosing a vortex,
there is a clockwise 3-cycle between the edges of
T
(so that the arrow corresponds to anticlockwise angles in
T
).

•

For each vortex
V
that is enclosed in a self-folded triangle
T

with self-folded edge
i
and loop edge
l
, we call these two edges twins.
Add an arrow between
i
and (from/to)
j
whenever there is an arrow between
l
and (from/to)
j
, for
j\neq l
in
\mathrm{T}
.
So besides the usual (type P-1) puzzle piece (i.e. a triangle without involving a vortex in a self-folded triangle–see any triangle in
Figure

7
),
there are three types of other puzzle pieces in a triangulation
\mathrm{T}
shown in
Figure

27
.

Figure 27.
The quivers with potential associated to types P-2/P-3/P-4 puzzle pieces with vortices

•

The unreduced potential is

\widehat{W_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}}=\sum_{T}W_{T}^{\text{\YinYang}}-\sum_{V}W_{V}^{\text{\YinYang}},

where

–

the first sum is over triangles which is not a self-folded triangle enclosing a vortex,
each team
W_{T}^{\text{\YinYang}}
is the sum of all corresponding 3-cycles (one can replacing an edge of
\mathrm{T}
with its twin).
This means in type P-
k
, there are
2^{k-1}
3-cycle terms in
W_{T}^{\text{\YinYang}}
for the non self-folded triangle
T
.

–

the second sum is over all vortices which are not enclosed in a self-folded triangle
T
,
each term
W_{V}^{\text{\YinYang}}
is an anticlockwise cycle around
V
, which needs to skip some of the arrows.
More precisely, if there are twins incident at
V
, which corresponding to another vortex
V_{0}
,
then

*

W_{V}^{\text{\YinYang}}
skip the self-folded edge at
V_{0}
and only count loop-edge once if
\epsilon(V_{0})=1
;

*

W_{V}^{\text{\YinYang}}
skip the loop-edge and only count self-folded edge if
\epsilon(V_{0})=-1
.

Definition B.3
.

Let
(Q_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}},W_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})
be the quiver with potential associated to a signed triangulation
\mathrm{T}^{\times}=(\mathrm{T},\epsilon)
of
\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}
, which is the reduced part of
(\widehat{Q_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}},\widehat{W_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}})
.

Example B.4
.

For instance, the term
W_{V}^{\text{\YinYang}}
in the potential is

•

a 5-cycle for the left picture of
Figure

28
;

•

a 10-cycle
7\to 8\to 4\to 1\to 2\to 2\to 1\to 3\to 5\to 6\to 7
for the middle picture of
Figure

28
;

•

a 7-cycle
7\to 8\to 3\to 1^{\epsilon(V)}\to 2\to 5\to 6\to 7
for the right picture of
Figure

28
,
where
\epsilon(V)
is the sign of the vortex
V_{0}
(in a self-folded triangle) adjacent to
V
.

Figure 28.
The extra term in the potential for each vortex (not in a self-folded triangle)

Figure

29
shows a reduction of quiver with potential,
where one needs to kill a 2-cycle around a vortex (and change the potential accordingly).
For more examples, see
[
L-F
]
.

Figure 29.
Reduction of a quiver with potential

Note that:

•

the quiver with potential is the one we considered in
Definition

2.8
if
\text{\YinYang}=\varnothing
;

•

the quiver with potential is non-degenerate if and only if
\text{\YinYang}=\text{\Sun}
; in which case,
it is the one constructed in
[
L-F
]
for classical cluster theory.

Lemma B.5
.

Any usual flip of signed triangulations induces mutations
between the associated quivers with potential (up to right-equivalence).

Proof.

This is a local issue. We only need to check the following.

•

For the usual mutation corresponding to flipping diagonals of a quadrilateral,
we know that the corresponding quivers with 3-cycles potential are related by mutation
up to right equivalent as below.

(B.4)

•

For the flip
\mu_{1}
in
Figure

7
, we cut the self-folded edge
(corresponding to vertex 2 in the two pictures in the middle)
and the mutation becomes the case above.
Conversely, by identifying vertex
2
and
5
in (
B.4
),
we obtain the mutation/flip
\mu_{1}
in
Figure

7
.

•

For other cases, we can always cut all the self-folded edge (provided the corresponding enclosed puncture is a not a vortex) as above, and reduce to the classical cluster case.
Then the lemma follows from
[
L-F
]
.
∎

The symmetry group

Denote by

(B.5)

\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})\ltimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}

the
signed mapping class group
of
\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}
,
where
\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,{\rightmoon}})
is the mapping class group of
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}

(that preserves
\mathbf{M},\leftmoon
and
{\rightmoon}
setwise, respectively)
and
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}
acts on the signs of vortices.

Note that we have rule out all the non-amenable cases (in the sense of
[
BS
, Def. 9.3]
)
by the assumptions in
Remark

B.1
.
Then we have the analogue of
[
BS
, Prop. 8.5 and Prop. 8.6]
.

Lemma B.6
.

Two signed triangulations
\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{i}
are in the same
\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
-orbit if and only if
the associated quivers
Q_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{i}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}
are isomorphic.
In particular,
\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
acts freely on
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.

Proof.

For the first claim, we only need prove the
if
part.
In other words, we can reconstruct the triangulation from the associated quiver.
In our case, a vertex
i
of
Q
with loops precisely corresponds to
a self-folded edge of the triangulation, whose corresponding isolated puncture is in
\leftmoon
.
Moreover, there is exactly one vertex
j\neq i
in
Q
that is incident with
i

(more precisely there is a 2-cycle between them).
Deleting all such vertices (with loops) from the quiver corresponds to cut the surface along all those self-folded edges.
Then the reconstruction of the triangulation of cut surface can be done by
[
BS
, Prop. 8.5]
.
More, one can add back those vertices and gluing back the original surface accordingly.
Thus, the first claim follows.

The second follows as
[
BS
, Prop. 8.6]
similarly.
∎

Remark B.7
.

We also have the following:
two signed triangulations are in the same
\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
-orbit if and only if
the associated quivers with potential are right-equivalent.
This is because the isomorphic class of quivers (associated to a fixed MSx)
and the right-equivalent class of quivers with potential (associated to the same fixed MSx)
determines each other.

B.2.
Deformed Calabi-Yau categories

As in
Section

3.2
,
let
\Gamma_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}
be the Ginzberg dg algebra of
(Q_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}},W_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})
and

\operatorname{pvd}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})
its the (Calabi-Yau-3) perfectly valued derived category with canonical heart
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}
.

Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
be a finite heart.
Recall that the
Ext quiver

\mathcal{Q}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})
is the (positively) graded quiver
whose vertices are the simples of
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
and
whose degree
k
arrows
S_{i}\to S_{j}
correspond to a basis of

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}}(S_{i},S_{j}[k])
.
Equivalently,
denote by
\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})
the dg-endomorphism algebra

\operatorname{\mathcal{E}nd}\left(\bigoplus_{S\in\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}}S\right)
for
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
.
Then
\mathcal{Q}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})
is the
Gabriel quiver
of
\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})
.
Further, for a graded quiver
Q
, define

•

the
Calabi-Yau-
N
double
,
denoted by
\overline{Q}^{N}
, to be the quiver obtained from
Q

by adding an arrow
j\to i
of degree
N-k
for each arrow
i\to j
of degree
k

and adding a loop of degree
N
at each vertex.

•

the grading shift dual, denote by
Q^{\vee}
, to be the same quiver with
grading changes
d\mapsto 1-d
.

The lemma below follows from the standard simple-projective duality.

Lemma B.8
.

The Ext-quiver of
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}
is the Calabi-Yau-3 double of
(Q_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})^{\vee}
,
i.e.

\mathcal{Q}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})=\overline{(Q_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})^{\vee}}^{3}.

Remark B.9
.

In fact, we can also read of the information of the associated potential
W_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}
from
\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})
,
cf.
[
Ke1
, App.A]
.
In other word, the right equivalent classes of QP determines and is determined
by the corresponding 3-Calabi-Yau category or its canonical heart, cf. proof of
[
BS
, Thm. 9.9]
.

B.3.
Triangle equivalences

We proceed to show that flips of signed triangulations induce triangle equivalences,
which is a slightly upgraded of
[
KY
]
.
Recall that an object
S
is
spherical
if

\operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}(S,S)=\mathbf{k}\oplus\mathbf{k}[-3]

and that it then induces a
spherical twist functor

\Phi_{S}
, defined by

\Phi_{S}(X)=\operatorname{Cone}\left(S\otimes\operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}(S,X)\to X\right).

Proposition B.10
.

For any flip of signed triangulation
\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{1}\xrightarrow{\gamma_{1}}\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{2}
,
there is a triangle equivalence

(B.6)

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\gamma_{1}}\colon\operatorname{pvd}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{1}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})\cong\operatorname{pvd}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{2}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})

satisfying

(B.7)

\Upsilon_{\gamma_{1}}^{-1}\left(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{2}}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}\right)=\left(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{1}}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}\right)^{\sharp}_{S_{\gamma_{1}}}.

Moreover, for a 2-cycle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{2}}
,
we have

\Upsilon_{\gamma_{2}}\circ\Upsilon_{\gamma_{1}}=\Phi^{-1}_{S_{\gamma_{1}}}
and
\Upsilon_{\gamma_{1}}\circ\Upsilon_{\gamma_{2}}=\Phi^{-1}_{S_{\gamma_{2}}}.

Proof.

If the flip is non self-folded, then the proposition follows from
[
KY
, Thm. 3.2]

(that mutation implies derived equivalence).
Thus, we only need to consider the L-flip case, where
\mathrm{T}_{1}=\mathrm{T}=\mathrm{T}_{2}
.

By Kozsul duality (e.g.
[
Qy2
, § 5.1]
),
\operatorname{pvd}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})
can be realized as
the perfect derived category of the dg algebra
\operatorname{per}\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})
.
Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}
the tilted heart in the right hand side of (
B.7
) and
we only need to show that
\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})\cong\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime})
.
For the local case, i.e.
\mu_{1}
for the QP in the rightmost picture of
Figure

7
,
we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})=\operatorname{\mathcal{E}nd}(S_{1}\oplus S_{2})\cong\operatorname{\mathcal{E}nd}(S_{1}[1]\oplus S_{2}^{\prime})=\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime})

by
[
CHQ
, Cor. 4.21]
.
Moreover,
S_{2}^{\prime}
admits a filtration with three factors
S_{1},S_{1}
and
S_{2}
(cf.
[
CHQ
, Ex. A.3]
).
In general,
S_{2}\in\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
is still the only simple that will change during
the forward tilting
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}\xrightarrow{S_{1}}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}
with the same formula (cf.
[
CHQ
, App. A.2]
)
and
S_{2}^{\prime}
is built by the same filtration as above.
As
\operatorname{Hom}(S_{1},S)=0
for any simple
S\in\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}\smallsetminus\{S_{1},S_{2}\}
,
hence there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{\mathcal{E}nd}(\bigoplus_{\begin{smallmatrix}S\in\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}\\
S\neq S_{1}\end{smallmatrix}}S)\cong\operatorname{\mathcal{E}nd}(\bigoplus_{\begin{smallmatrix}S\in\operatorname{Sim}\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}\\
S\neq S_{1}[1]\end{smallmatrix}}S).

Patching together these isomorphisms, we obtain the required one
\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}})\cong\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime})
.
∎

Remark B.11
.

There is an autoequivalence of
\operatorname{pvd}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})

associated to each of the simple of
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}
.
More precisely, if the simple corresponds to a vertex of
Q_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}
without loops,
then it is spherical and induces a spherical twist;
if it corresponds to a vertex with loops, then there is an autoequivalence
\Upsilon_{\gamma}
in (
B.6
) constructed as above.

By abuse of notations, we denote
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{pvd}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})
, where
\mathrm{T}
is the initial triangulation.
So
\{\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\}
, for various partition
\text{\Sun}=\leftmoon\cup\text{\YinYang}
,
are a class of (Calabi-Yau) deformations of
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
.
The fully deformed one, denoted by
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})
, is the one studied in
[
BS
]
.

Remark B.12
.

In DMSp case, we can identified
\operatorname{pvd}(\Gamma_{\mathbb{T}}^{\text{\Sun}})
consistently for any
\mathbb{T}\in\operatorname{FG}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
by
Theorem

3.7
.
In DMSx case, we are not sure if we can do the same at the moment.
Without decorations, one certainly can not identify
\operatorname{pvd}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}})
consistently for all
\mathrm{T}^{\times}\in\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})

(as there are monodromies).
For instance, the spherical twists in
Proposition

B.10
are non-trivial monodromies.

Denote by
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
the principal component of the exchange graph of hearts of
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
Let
\mathcal{A}ut^{\circ}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
be the group of autoequivalences of
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
,
consisting of those that preserve
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})

and
\operatorname{Ng}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
its subgroup consisting of
negligible
ones, i.e. acting trivially on
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
So what we really need is the their quotient:

(B.8)

\operatorname{Aut}^{\circ}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\colon=\mathcal{A}ut^{\circ}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})/\operatorname{Ng}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}).

B.4.
Flip graphs as exchange graphs

In this section, we prove a quotient/weak version of (
3.4
) for
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.

Take any heart
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
in
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
,
then there is a simple tilting sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}=\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{m}=\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}.}

By
Proposition

B.10
, it corresponds to flip sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\times}=\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{m}}

in
\operatorname{FG}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
so that
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
is equivalent to the standard heart
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{i}}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}
.
This implies that for each heart in
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
:

•

there is an associated quivers with potential (up to right-equivalence);

•

there are
n
associated autoequivalences of
\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
corresponding to its simples by
Remark

B.11
.

Denote by
\mathcal{M}T(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
the autoequivalence group generated by all such associated autoequivalences
and
\operatorname{MT}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
its image in
\operatorname{Aut}^{\circ}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.

Proposition B.13
.

There is a natural isomorphism

(B.9)

\displaystyle\operatorname{X}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}_{*}\colon\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})/\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))/\operatorname{Aut}^{\circ},

sending the
\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
-orbit of a signed triangulation
\mathrm{T}^{\times}

to the
\operatorname{Aut}^{\circ}\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
-orbit of the corresponding heart
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}
.

We expect that
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))/\operatorname{MT}
as analogue of
[
KQ2
, Thm. 2.10]

(=
[
Ke2
, Thm. 5.6]
), which requires further studies.

Proof.

Fix a signed triangulation
\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{0}
in
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
and
make a spanning tree
\operatorname{Tr}_{0}
(which is a subgraph with the same vertex set and is also a tree) of
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.

For each
\mathrm{T}^{\times}
in
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
, let
p
be the unique path in
\operatorname{Tr}_{0}
connecting
\mathrm{T}^{\times}
and
\mathrm{T}^{\times}_{0}
.
By
Proposition

B.10
, there is tilting sequence induced by
\operatorname{Tr}_{0}
so that we can associated a heart
X(\mathrm{T}^{\times})=\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\times}}^{{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}
to
\mathrm{T}^{\times}
.
Therefore, we obtain a graph
X(\operatorname{Tr}_{0})
consisting of hearts in
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
with simple tilting and
and a natural (graph) isomorphism
X\colon\operatorname{Tr}_{0}\to X(\operatorname{Tr}_{0})
.

Next, we replace each heart
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}
in
X(\operatorname{Tr}_{0})
by its
\operatorname{MT}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
-orbit
[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}]
and complete the graph
into
\overline{X(\operatorname{Tr}_{0})}
by adding edges
[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{1}]\to[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{2}]
whenever there is a simple forward tilting
from a representative of
[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{1}]
to a representative of
[\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{2}]
.
We claim that the isomorphism
X
extend to
X\colon\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\to\overline{X(\operatorname{Tr}_{0})}
.
By
Proposition

B.10
, each edge in
X(\operatorname{Tr}_{0})
will be doubled to be a 2-cycle that corresponds to the 2-cycle of
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
Moreover, there are (oriented) squares and pentagons in
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
as in
[
KQ2
, Lem. 2.12]
,
which correspond to squares and pentagons in
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
Hence, by
Proposition

B.2
, we can partially complete
\operatorname{Tr}_{0}
to match all non-loops edges of
\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
Furthermore,
Proposition

B.10
implies that we also need to add the corresponding loops
(for those auto-equivalences induced by L-flips) in
\overline{X(\operatorname{Tr}_{0})}
.
Thus we have already arrived at an injection
X\colon\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\to\overline{X(\operatorname{Tr}_{0})}
.
It is in fact an isomorphism since both graphs are
(n,n)
-regular, as claimed.

Finally, it is clear that
\operatorname{MT}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
is a subgroup of
\operatorname{Aut}^{\circ}
and hence
\overline{X(\operatorname{Tr}_{0})}

is a covering of
\operatorname{EG}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))/\operatorname{Aut}^{\circ}
.
Namely, orbits of hearts in
\overline{X(\operatorname{Tr}_{0})}
could be further identified under
\operatorname{Aut}^{\circ}
.
To show that
X
induces the required isomorphism, we only need to show that
two hearts
\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{1},\operatorname{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
are related by
\operatorname{Aut}^{\circ}
if and only if the corresponding triangulation
\mathrm{T}_{1}
and
\mathrm{T}_{2}
are related by an element in
\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
.
The former condition implies the associated QPs are right-equivalent (cf.
Remark

B.9
)
and hence implies the latter condition by
Remark

B.7
.
The inverse direction is straightforward.
∎

B.5.
Categorification

As in
Section

6.2
,
\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
admits the same style of stratification and cell structure.
Note that the cells will correspond to sign triangulations of
\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}
.
Moreover, when there are self-folded with a vortex as isolated puncture,
one needs to change the local coordinate for the cell, cf.
[
BS
, § 10]
.
More precisely, instead of using the periods
z_{i}
of the actual saddle connections
\eta_{i}
as shown in
Figure

30
, one replace
z_{2}
with
z_{1}+z_{2}
.
In this way,
z_{2}
is allowed to be zero, which corresponds to the collision of the vortex with the simple zero in the self-folded triangle.

Then we have an analogue of
Lemma

6.5
, for the
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}
-framed version, i.e. (
6.8
) and (
6.9
).

Lemma B.14
.

There is a canonical embedding

(B.10)

\wp_{\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}}\colon\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\to\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})

whose image is dual to
B_{2}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
and which induces a surjective map

(B.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\wp_{*}\colon\pi_{1}\operatorname{FG}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}).}

[
BS
, Thm. 11.2]
,
we can upgrade the graph isomorphism (
B.9
) into the following isomorphism between orbifolds.

Theorem B.15
.

There is an isomorphism between complex orbifolds

\operatorname{Quad}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\cong\operatorname{Stab}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))/\operatorname{Aut}^{\circ}

Appendix C
Examples: Euclidean Artin braid groups

C.1.
Artin braid groups and Coxeter groups

Let
\widetilde{\Omega}
a diagram (without loops) with an (edge-) weight function
\mathbf{v}\colon\widetilde{\Omega}_{1}\to\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 3}
.

Definition C.1
.

The Artin braid group
\operatorname{Br}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}
associated to
\widetilde{\Omega}
is defined by the presentation

(C.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{\Omega})\colon=\langle b_{i}\mid i\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{0}\rangle\Big/(\operatorname{Br}^{m_{ij}}(b_{i},b_{j})\mid\forall i,j\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{0}),

where

m_{ij}=\begin{cases}2,&\mbox{if there is no edge beteween $i$ and $j$};\\
\mathbf{v}(\alpha),&\mbox{if there is exactly one edge $i\frac{\alpha}{\hskip 8.19447pt}j$};\\
\infty,&\mbox{if there are multi edges between $i$ and $j$}.\end{cases}

The associated Coxeter/Weyl group
W_{\widetilde{\Omega}}
is the quotient group of
\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{\Omega})

by adding the square to one relation (i.e.
b_{i}^{2}=1
for any
i\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{1}
) to the presentation.
It is well-known that there are four infinity families of Euclidean Artin braid groups,
i.e. affine types
\widetilde{A},\widetilde{B},\widetilde{C}
and
\widetilde{D}
.
In this section, we show that all of them are arise as fundamental groups of
moduli spaces of framed quadratic differentials studied above.

C.2.
Hyperplane arrangements

We restrict
\widetilde{\Omega}
to ba a Euclidean Dynkin diagram of
n+1
vertices
and
\Omega
be the corresponding finite Dynkin diagram.
Denote by
\Phi_{\Omega}
the root system associated to
\Omega
, living in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and

\Phi_{\widetilde{\Omega}}=\Phi_{\Omega}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\langle\delta\rangle
the root system associated to
\widetilde{\Omega}
living in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{R}_{\delta}
,
where
\delta
is the basic imaginary root.
Denote by
\mathfrak{h}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}^{*}=\mathfrak{h}^{*}_{\Omega}\oplus\mathbb{C}_{\delta}
the complexification of
\mathbb{R}^{n}\oplus\mathbb{R}_{\delta}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\cong\mathbb{C}^{n+1}
its dual complex space.
Each root
(\alpha,k\delta)
in
\Phi_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}^{*}
determines an affine hyperplane

H_{\alpha,k}=\{\varsigma\in\mathfrak{h}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\mid\langle\varsigma,\alpha\rangle=k\}

and let
\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\subset\mathfrak{h}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}
be the complement of the these affine hyperplanes, i.e.

(C.2)

\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\colon=\mathfrak{h}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\smallsetminus\big(\bigcup_{(\alpha,k\delta)\in\Phi_{\widetilde{\Omega}}}H_{\alpha,k}\big).

This is the space of hyperplane arrangements and
it is well-known that
W_{\widetilde{\Omega}}
can be geometrically realized as the reflection group on
\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}
,
where the generators
r_{s_{i}}
are the reflections with respect to simple roots of
\widetilde{\Omega}
. Moreover,
W_{\widetilde{\Omega}}
acts freely on
\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}
and one has

\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}/W_{\widetilde{\Omega}})=\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{\Omega}).

A recent breakthrough of
[
PS
]
proves the
K(\pi,1)
-conjecture for Euclidean braid groups.

Theorem C.2
.

[
PS
]

The universal cover of
\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}
is contractible.

For the later use, we recall some facts from
[
All
]
.
Let

(C.3)

\displaystyle V_{0}=\{\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{C}^{n}\mid x_{j}\neq\pm x_{k},\forall j\neq k\}

and

(C.4)

\begin{cases}V_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}=\{\mathbf{x}\in V_{0}\mid\forall x_{j}\notin\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\},\\
V_{\widetilde{B_{n}}}=\{\mathbf{x}\in V_{0}\mid\forall x_{j}\notin\mathbb{Z}\},\\
V_{\widetilde{D_{n}}}=V_{0}.\end{cases}

Then there are homotopy equivalences
V_{\widetilde{\Omega}}/W_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{\Omega}}/W_{\widetilde{\Omega}}

for
\widetilde{\Omega}=\widetilde{C_{n}},\widetilde{B_{n}}
or
\widetilde{D_{n}}
.

Remark C.3
.

Consider the following map

\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\iota\colon\mathbb{C}&\xrightarrow{e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}(?)}}&\mathbb{C}^{*}&\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\eta\hskip 8.19447pt}&\mathbb{C}^{0,1},\\
x&\mapsto&y&\mapsto&\frac{1}{4}(1-y)(1-\frac{1}{y}),\end{array}

where
\mathbb{C}^{0,1}=\mathbb{C}\smallsetminus\{0,1\}

\eta
is a branched double cover, branching at
0,1
(whose preimages are
\pm 1
).
It induces a homotopy equivalence

(C.5)

V_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}/W_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}\simeq\operatorname{conf}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{0,1}),

where
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
is permutation group of
n
elements.

When passing from
V_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}
to
V_{\widetilde{B_{n}}}
,
one adds hyperplanes

(C.6)

H^{1}_{j,m}=\{\ x_{j}=\frac{1}{2}+m\}\cap V_{0},\qquad m\in\mathbb{Z},

that corresponds to partial compactification

\overline{\operatorname{conf}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{0,1})}^{1}
of
\operatorname{conf}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{0,1})

by allowing one of the points in the configuration goes to
1
.

When passing from
V_{\widetilde{B_{n}}}
to
V_{\widetilde{D_{n}}}
,
one adds more hyperplanes

(C.7)

H^{0}_{k,m}=\{\ x_{k}=m\}\cap V_{0},\qquad m\in\mathbb{Z},

that corresponds to further partial compactification
\overline{\operatorname{conf}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{0,1})}^{0,1}

by allowing one of the points in the configuration goes to
0
as well.

C.3.
Affine type
\widetilde{A_{n}}

Take a partition
n=p+q
for some positive integers
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The diagram
\widetilde{\Omega}=\widetilde{A_{p,q}}=\widetilde{A_{n}}
is an
(n+1)
-cycle.
Let
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}
is an annulus (with
\mathbf{P}=\varnothing
) with

p
and
q
marked points in two boundaries of
\mathbf{S}
respectively.
For simplicity, assume that
p\neq q
.

The standard generators of
\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S})
are the clockwise rotations along one of the boundaries, moving a marked point to the next adjacent one, denoted by
r_{0}
and
r_{\infty}
respectively.
They satisfy
r_{0}^{p}=D=r_{\infty}^{q}
, where
D
is the unique Dehn twist along either boundary of
\mathbf{S}
, and fit into a short exact sequence (with an asymmetric choice between
p
and
q
)

1\to\mathbb{Z}\langle r_{0}\rangle\to\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S})\to\mathbb{Z}_{q}\langle r_{\infty}\rangle\to 1.

Moreover,
\operatorname{Quad}(\mathbf{S})
consists of differentials of the form

\phi(z)=\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}(z-z_{i})}{z^{p+2}}dz^{2},\quad z_{i}\in\mathbb{C}^{*},\quad z_{i}\neq z_{j}

and considered modulo the action of
\mathbb{C}
rescaling
z
.
Then there is a commutative diagram

(C.8)

\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S})
\operatorname{conf}_{n+1}(\mathbb{C}^{*})
\operatorname{Quad}(\mathbf{S})
.
\mathbb{Z}
\operatorname{MCG}(\mathbf{S})
\mathbb{Z}_{q}

By
[
Qy2
, § 8.3]
,
the the contractible space

\operatorname{Stab}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}))\cong\operatorname{FQuad}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta})
is the universal cover of

\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S})=\operatorname{FQuad}(0;1^{p+q},-p-2,-q-2)\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{A_{n}}}/W_{\widetilde{A_{n}}}

with covering group

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S})=\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta})=\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{A_{p,q}}).

The short exact sequence (
2.13
) in
Theorem

2.6
becomes

(C.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{SBr}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\bf H}_{1}(\mathbf{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}\operatorname{conf}_{n+1}(\mathbb{C}^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}}

C.4.
Affine type
\widetilde{C_{n}}

The diagram
\widetilde{\Omega}=\widetilde{C_{n}}
is shown in the right diagram in
Figure

31
(for
n=5
).
Consider a DMSp
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
, which is an
(n-2)
-gon with two punctures
\text{\Sun}=\{P_{0},P_{1}\}
and
n
decorations.

Figure 31.
A (triangulated) DMSp
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
for Artin braid group of type
\widetilde{C_{n=5}}
.

Up to translation and scaling (that fixes two double poles to be 0 and 1),

\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
consists of differentials of the form

\phi(z)=\lambda\cdot\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{n}(z-z_{i})}{z^{2}(z-1)^{2}}dz^{2},\quad\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{*},z_{i}\in\mathbb{C}^{0,1},\quad z_{i}\neq z_{j}.

Hence,
\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})
is a
\mathbb{C}_{\lambda}^{*}
-bundle over
\operatorname{conf}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{0,1})
,
which is a homotopy equivalent to
\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}/W_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}
by (
C.5
):

(C.10)

\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})\xrightarrow[\backslash\mathbb{C}_{\lambda}^{*}]{\simeq}\operatorname{conf}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{0,1})\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}/W_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}.

The the contractible space
\operatorname{Stab}^{\circ}(\operatorname{\mathcal{D}}_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}))\cong\operatorname{FQuad}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
is the universal cover of

\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})=\operatorname{FQuad}(0;1^{n},(-2)^{2},-n-2),

with covering group

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})=\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{C_{n}}).

C.5.
Affine type
\widetilde{B_{n}}

The diagram
\widetilde{\Omega}=\widetilde{B_{n}}
is shown in the right diagram in
Figure

32
(for
n=5
).
Keep the DMSp
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
as above in
Section

C.4
.
Consider the DMSx
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}
by taking the partition
\text{\Sun}=\leftmoon\cup{\rightmoon}
for
\leftmoon=\{P_{0}\}
and
{\rightmoon}=\{P_{1}\}
.

Figure 32.
A (triangulated) DMSx
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}
for Artin braid group of type
\widetilde{B_{n=5}}
.

We have a moduli space

(C.11)

\begin{array}[]{rl}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=&\operatorname{FQuad}(0;1^{n},(-2)^{2},-n-2)\\
\bigsqcup&\operatorname{FQuad}(0;1^{n-1},-1,-2,-n-2)^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\end{array}

with

(C.12)

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{C_{n}})/\operatorname{L}^{2}({\rightmoon})=\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{B_{n}})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

The notation
?^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
means that there is a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-orbifolding for the extra strata.
Equivalently, (
C.12
) can be rewritten as

(C.13)

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})=\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{B_{n}}).

By
Remark

C.3
,
\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})
is a
\mathbb{C}_{\lambda}^{*}
-bundle over
the partial compactification
\overline{\operatorname{conf}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{0,1})}^{1}
,
that the extra strata corresponds to hyperplanes in (
C.6
).
These hyperplanes are fixed points under the corresponding reflections
and hence get
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifolding in the
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
-quotient,
i.e. we have

(C.14)

\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\xrightarrow[\backslash\mathbb{C}_{\lambda}^{*}]{\simeq}\overline{\operatorname{conf}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{0,1})}^{1}\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{B_{n}}}/W_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}.

On the other hand,
\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{C_{n}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})
and

\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{B_{n}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})=\frac{\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}},{\leftmoon})}{\operatorname{L}^{2}({\rightmoon})\cap\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}},{\leftmoon})}.

Then the top right short exact sequence (in red) in
Figure

23
induces the following short exact sequence

(C.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\bf H}_{1}(\text{\YinYang})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\widetilde{B_{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces/?^{2}=1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces/?^{2}=1}

where
/?^{2}=1
means taking quotient by (the normal subgroup generated by) squares of their standard generators.
Thus (
C.14
) is equivalent to

\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\simeq\left(\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{B_{n}}}/W_{\widetilde{B_{n}}}\right)\big/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{B_{n}}}/W_{\widetilde{B_{n}}}.

C.6.
Affine type
\widetilde{D_{n}}

The diagram
\widetilde{\Omega}=\widetilde{D_{n}}
is shown in the right diagram in
Figure

33
(for
n=5
).
Keep the DMSp
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}}
as above in
Section

C.4
.
Consider the DMSv
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\YinYang}}
by taking the partition
\text{\Sun}=\leftmoon\cup{\rightmoon}
for
\leftmoon=\varnothing
and
{\rightmoon}=\text{\Sun}
.

Figure 33.
A (triangulated) DMSv
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\YinYang}}
for Artin braid group of type
\widetilde{D_{n=5}}
.

There is a moduli space

(C.16)

\begin{array}[]{rl}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})=&\operatorname{FQuad}(0;1^{n},(-2)^{2},-n-2)\\
\bigsqcup&\operatorname{FQuad}(0;1^{n-1},-1,-2,-n-2)^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\\
\bigsqcup&\operatorname{FQuad}(0;1^{n-2},(-1)^{2},-n-2)^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\oplus 2}}\end{array}

with

(C.17)

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{C_{n}})/\operatorname{L}^{2}(\text{\Sun})=\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{D_{n}})\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\oplus 2}}.

Equivalently, (
C.17
) can be rewritten as

(C.18)

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}^{\varnothing,\text{\Sun}})=\operatorname{Br}(\widetilde{D_{n}}).

In this case,
\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}^{\varnothing,\text{\Sun}})
is also isomorphic to the cluster braid group of type
\widetilde{D_{n}}
,
in the sense of King-Qiu
[
KQ2
]
.

Similar to type
{\widetilde{B_{n}}}
, we have

•

There is a homotopy equivalence

(C.19)

\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})\xrightarrow[\backslash\mathbb{C}_{\lambda}^{*}]{\simeq}\overline{\operatorname{conf}_{n}(\mathbb{C}^{0,1})}^{0,1}\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{D_{n}}}/W_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}.

•

The top right short exact sequence (in red) in
Figure

23
induces the following:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\widetilde{D_{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\widetilde{C_{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\oplus 2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1.}

•

There are homotopy equivalences

\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})\simeq\left(\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{D_{n}}}/W_{\widetilde{D_{n}}}\right)\big/\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\oplus 2}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\widetilde{D_{n}}}/W_{\widetilde{D_{n}}}.

C.7.
Spherical Artin braid groups

We also list the surfaces for the four infinity families of spherical Artin braid groups
\Omega
.
Note that in this case the space of hyperplane arrangements is

(C.20)

\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\Omega}\colon=\mathfrak{h}_{\Omega}\smallsetminus\big(\bigcup_{\alpha\in\Phi_{\Omega}}H_{\alpha}\big)

for
H_{\alpha}=\{\varsigma\in\mathfrak{h}_{\lambda}\mid\langle\varsigma,\alpha\rangle=0\}
.

•

For an
(n+3)
-gon
\mathbf{S}
with no punctures,
one has that
\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S})\cong\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{A_{n}}/W_{A_{n}}
and

\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S})=\operatorname{BT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta})=\operatorname{Br}(A_{n}).

•

For an
n
-gon
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}}
with one puncture,
one has that
\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S})\cong\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{B_{n}}/W_{B_{n}}
and

\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\Sun}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\text{\Sun}})=\operatorname{Br}(C_{n}).

•

For an
n
-gon
\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}}
with one vortex,
one has that
\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S})\cong\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{D_{n}}/W_{D_{n}}
and

\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})=\operatorname{Br}(D_{n}).

All the statements in the affine cases also hold here.

C.8.
Summary

Let
♈
be a non-exceptional finite/affine Dynkin diagram
and
\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}
be the corresponding DMSx mentioned above.
Combining all the discussion above,
we obtain the following geometric realization of hyperplane arrangements
\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\text{\Aries}}/W_{\text{\Aries}}
.

Theorem C.4
.

There is a homotopy equivalence

\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})\simeq\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\text{\Aries}}/W_{\text{\Aries}}

whose fundamental group fits into the following commutative diagram:

(C.21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{SBr}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\bf H}_{1}(\mathbf{S}^{\text{\YinYang}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}^{\pm}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}\operatorname{conf}_{\Delta}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\bf H}_{1}(\mathbf{S})\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{{\oplus\text{\YinYang}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{AJ}^{\text{\YinYang}}}

where
\operatorname{AJ}^{\text{\YinYang}}
is the vortex version of the AJ map.
In the ‘lattice’ case (i.e. finite Dynkin,
\widetilde{A}_{n}
or
\widetilde{C}_{n}
, cf.
[
McC
]
),
a contractible universal cover of
\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\text{\Aries}}/W_{\text{\Aries}}
is given by
\operatorname{Stab}^{\circ}(D_{3}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}}))
.

Proof.

As

\operatorname{Br}(\text{\Aries})=\pi_{1}(\mathfrak{h}^{\operatorname{reg}}_{\text{\Aries}}/W_{\text{\Aries}})=\pi_{1}\operatorname{FQuad}(\mathbf{S}^{\leftmoon,\text{\YinYang}})=\operatorname{MT}(\mathbf{S}_{\Delta}^{\leftmoon/{\rightmoon}^{2}}),

the top short exact sequence is precisely (
7.9
).

Then we only need to clarify the last statement:

•

The finite Dynkin case follows from
[
QW
, Thm. B]
.

•

The
\widetilde{A}_{n}
and
\widetilde{C}_{n}
case follows from
Theorem

1.2
and
the contractibility of
Theorem

C.2
(or
[
Oko
]
in these two cases).

∎
